{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
,
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
are all different. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
and
f_{1}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{2}
and
\eta_{2}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{1}
and
\eta_{2}
. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq d^{*}+\beta\,d_{1}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle~~~+d_{2}+\beta(d^{*}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{\rho}}\cdot(d^{*}+d_{1}))-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle~~~+d_{1}+\beta(d^{*}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{\rho}}\cdot(d^{*}+d_{1}))-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=\boxed{(1+2\beta+\nicefrac{{2\beta}}{{\rho}})\,d^{*}-(2-\beta-\nicefrac{{2\beta}}{{\rho}})\,d_{1}+(1-3\beta)\,d_{2}}.

Tree swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{1}

On
\mathcal{T}_{11}\cap\mathcal{A}
,
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
is the only swap closing any facility in
\{f_{1},f_{2}\}
by the definition of
\mathcal{T}_{11}
. In other words, both
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
and
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
coincide with
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
as long as they exist.
Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{11}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}}.

On
\mathcal{T}_{12}\cap\mathcal{A}
, we have
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}\neq{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
by implication (ii) of amenability. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{1}
and
\pi(f_{2})
by implication (Tii) of amenability. We have
d(c,\pi(f_{2}))\leq 2d_{2}+d^{*}
by (
3
). Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{12}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle~~~+d_{1}+\beta(2\,d_{2}+d^{*})-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta+\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}}.

Tree swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{2}

On
\mathcal{T}_{21}\cap\mathcal{A}
, we have
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}\neq{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
and
f_{1}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
\eta_{2}
and
\pi(f_{1})
by implications (ii) and (Tii) of amenability. We have
d(c,\eta_{2})\leq d^{*}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{\rho}}\cdot(d^{*}+d_{1})
and
d(c,\pi(f_{1}))\leq 2d_{1}+d^{*}
. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{21}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq d^{*}+\beta\,d_{1}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle~~~+(d^{*}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{\rho}}(d^{*}+d_{1}))+\beta(2d_{1}+d^{*})-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=\boxed{(2+\beta+\nicefrac{{1}}{{\rho}})\,d^{*}-(2-3\beta-\nicefrac{{1}}{{\rho}})\,d_{1}-2\beta\,d_{2}}.

On
\mathcal{T}_{22}\cap\mathcal{A}
, we first consider the case where all three swaps
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle},{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle},{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
are different.
On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{2}
and
\pi(f_{1})
(at distance
\leq 2d_{1}+d^{*}
), by implication (Tii) of amenability.
On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{1}
and
\pi(f_{2})
(at distance
\leq 2d_{2}+d^{*}
), again by implication (Tii) of amenability. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{22}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle~~~+d_{2}+\beta(2d_{1}+d^{*})-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle~~~+d_{1}+\beta(2d_{2}+d^{*})-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta+2\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-2\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-\beta)\,d_{2}}.

Since our bound for
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}}(c)
doesn’t require either
f_{1}
or
f_{2}
to remain open after the swap,
and both
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
and
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
are non-negative,
the above bound also holds when
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
and/or
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
coincides with
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
.

Summarizing, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta+\beta+\nicefrac{{\beta}}{{\rho}})\,d^{*}-(1-\nicefrac{{\beta}}{{\rho}})\,d_{1}-2\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=(1.8+0.2/\rho)\,d^{*}-(1-0.2/\rho)\,d_{1}-0.4\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+2\beta+\nicefrac{{2\beta}}{{\rho}})\,d^{*}-(2-\beta-\nicefrac{{2\beta}}{{\rho}})\,d_{1}+(1-3\beta)\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=(1.4+0.4/\rho)\,d^{*}-(1.8-0.4/\rho)\,d_{1}+0.4\,d_{2}

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{11}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.6\,d^{*}-d_{1}-0.2\,d_{2}

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{12}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta+\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}

\displaystyle=1.8\,d^{*}-d_{1}

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{21}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(2+\beta+\nicefrac{{1}}{{\rho}})\,d^{*}-(2-3\beta-\nicefrac{{1}}{{\rho}})\,d_{1}-2\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=(2.2+\nicefrac{{1}}{{\rho}})\,d^{*}-(1.4-\nicefrac{{1}}{{\rho}})\,d_{1}-0.4\,d_{2}

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{22}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta+2\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-2\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-\beta)\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=2\,d^{*}-1.6\,d_{1}+0.8\,d_{2}

We now combine these bounds to show an upper bound for
{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)
using
Lemma

D.1
. Note that our bound for
{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{11}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
is smaller than our bound for
{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{12}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
, so we only need to consider cases where the maximum in
Lemma

D.1
is attained at either
{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{11}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{21}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
or
{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{12}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{22}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
.

When
\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}<\rho(f^{*})\leq\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}
, we have
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}
and
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}]=0
. Therefore, if the maximum in
Lemma

D.1
is attained at
{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{11}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{21}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
, we have

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot({\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{11}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{21}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c))+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(1.85+0.35/\rho)d^{*}-(1.1-0.35/\rho)d_{1}-0.35d_{2}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

(
d_{2}\geq d_{1}
)

\displaystyle\leq(1.85+0.35\times\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}})d^{*}-(1.45-0.35\times\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}})d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle=\boxed{2.375d^{*}-0.925d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

If the maximum in
Lemma

D.1
is attained at
{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{12}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{22}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
, we have

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot({\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{12}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{22}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c))+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(1.85+0.1/\rho)d^{*}-(1.15-0.1/\rho)d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(1.85+0.1\times\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}})d^{*}-(1.15-0.1\times\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}})d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle=\boxed{2\,d^{*}-d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

When
\rho(f^{*})>\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}
, we have
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}]=\nicefrac{{5}}{{4}}-\rho
and
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}]=\rho-\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}
. Therefore, if the maximum in
Lemma

D.1
is attained at
{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{11}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{21}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
, we have

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq(\nicefrac{{5}}{{4}}-\rho)\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+(\rho-\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot({\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{11}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{21}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c))+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(2.35+0.2/\rho-0.4\rho)d^{*}-(0.3-0.2/\rho+0.8\rho)d_{1}-(0.95-0.8\rho)d_{2}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

(
d_{2}\geq d_{1}
)

\displaystyle\leq(2.35+0.2/\rho-0.4\rho)d^{*}-(1.25-0.2/\rho)d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(2.35+0.2\times\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}-0.4\times\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})d^{*}-(1.25-0.2\times\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}})d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{2.31667\,d^{*}-0.98333\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

If the maximum in
Lemma

D.1
is attained at
{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{12}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{22}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
, we have

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq(\nicefrac{{5}}{{4}}-\rho)\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+(\rho-\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot({\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{12}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{22}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c))+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(2.35-0.05/\rho-0.4\rho)d^{*}-(0.35+0.05/\rho+0.8\rho)d_{1}+(0.8\rho-0.6)d_{2}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

(
d_{2}\leq d^{*}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{\rho}}\cdot(d^{*}+d_{1})
)

\displaystyle\leq(2.55-0.65/\rho+0.4\rho)d^{*}-(0.65/\rho+0.8\rho-0.45)d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(2.55-0.65+0.4)d^{*}-(0.65\cdot\nicefrac{{4}}{{\sqrt{13}}}+0.8\cdot\nicefrac{{\sqrt{13}}}{{4}}-0.45)d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{2.3\,d^{*}-0.99222\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

D.3
Proof of (
6.19
): Close Clients of Type
\mathsf{B}

In this section, we show that for any close case client
c
with type
\mathsf{B}
, we have

\Delta_{\mathcal{A}}({c})\leq 2.4\,d^{*}(c)-0.9\,d_{1}(c).

In our type
\mathsf{A}
analysis, we further partitioned the tree events
\mathcal{T}_{1}
and
\mathcal{T}_{2}
as
\mathcal{T}_{1}=\mathcal{T}_{11}\cup\mathcal{T}_{12}
and
\mathcal{T}_{2}=\mathcal{T}_{21}\cup\mathcal{T}_{22}
.
We require this partitioning also in our type
\mathsf{B}
analysis,
with the roles of
\eta_{1}
and
\eta_{2}
flipped.
Specifically, we define
\mathcal{T}_{11}
as the intersection of
\mathcal{T}_{1}
and the event that there is a swap which closes both
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
but does not open the original copy of
f^{*}
. We define
\mathcal{T}_{21}
as the intersection of
\mathcal{T}_{2}
and the event that
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
is the only swap closing any facility in
\{f_{1},f_{2}\}
. We define
\mathcal{T}_{12}
and
\mathcal{T}_{22}
accordingly as
\mathcal{T}_{12}=\mathcal{T}_{1}\backslash\mathcal{T}_{11}
and
\mathcal{T}_{22}=\mathcal{T}_{2}\backslash\mathcal{T}_{21}
. Similar to
Lemma

D.1
, we have the following lemma for type
\mathsf{B}
:

Lemma D.2

(Type
\mathsf{B}
averaging)
.

For a close client of type
\mathsf{B}
with
\rho(f^{*})>\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}
, we have

{\Delta}_{\mathcal{T}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot\max\{{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{11}\cap\mathcal{A}}+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{21}\cap\mathcal{A}},{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{12}\cap\mathcal{A}}+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{21}\cap\mathcal{A}},{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{12}\cap\mathcal{A}}+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{22}\cap\mathcal{A}}\}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

We now proceed to bound the worst-case potential changes in different events.

Simple swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{1}

By implications (Siii) and (Siv), all three swaps
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle},{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle},{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
are different. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
and
f_{1}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{2}
and
\eta_{1}
by implication (Siii) of amenability. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{1}
and
\eta_{1}
, again by implication (Siii) of amenability. Note that
d(c,\eta_{1})\leq d^{*}+\rho(d^{*}+d_{1})
by (
3
). Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq d^{*}+\beta\,d_{1}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle~~~+d_{2}+\beta(d^{*}+\rho(d^{*}+d_{1}))-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle~~~+d_{1}+\beta(d^{*}+\rho(d^{*}+d_{1}))-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=\boxed{(1+2\beta+2\rho\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-\beta-2\rho\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-3\beta)\,d_{2}}.

Simple swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{2}

By implications (ii) and (Siv), we have
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}\neq{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{1}
and
\eta_{1}
, by implication (Siii) of amenability. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle~~~+d_{1}+\beta(d^{*}+\rho(d^{*}+d_{1}))-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta+\beta+\rho\beta)\,d^{*}-(1-\rho\beta)\,d_{1}-2\beta\,d_{2}}.

Tree swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{1}

On
\mathcal{T}_{11}\cap\mathcal{A}
, we have
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}\neq{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
and
f_{1}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
\eta_{1}
and
\pi(f_{1})
by implication (ii) and (Tii) of amenability. We have
d(c,\eta_{1})\leq d^{*}+\rho(d^{*}+d_{1})
and
d(c,\pi(f_{1}))\leq 2d_{1}+d^{*}
by (
2
). Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{11}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq d^{*}+\beta\,d_{1}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle~~~+(d^{*}+\rho(d^{*}+d_{1}))+\beta(2d_{1}+d^{*})-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{(2+\beta+\rho)\,d^{*}-(2-3\beta-\rho)\,d_{1}-2\beta\,d_{2}}.

On
\mathcal{T}_{12}\cap\mathcal{A}
, we first consider the case where all three swaps
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle},{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle},{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
are different. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{2}
and
\eta_{1}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{1}
and
\eta_{1}
. After both
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
and
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
,
\eta_{1}
is open by implication (ii) of amenability. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{12}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle~~~+d_{2}+\beta(d^{*}+\rho(d^{*}+d_{1}))-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle~~~+d_{1}+\beta(d^{*}+\rho(d^{*}+d_{1}))-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta+2\beta+2\rho\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-2\rho\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-3\beta)\,d_{2}}.

Since our bound for
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}}(c)
doesn’t require either
f_{1}
or
f_{2}
to remain open after the swap, the above bound also holds when
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
and/or
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
coincides with
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
.

Tree swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{2}

On
\mathcal{T}_{21}\cap\mathcal{A}
,
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
is the only swap closing any facility in
\{f_{1},f_{2}\}
by the definition of
\mathcal{T}_{21}
. In other words, both
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
and
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
coincide with
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
as long as they exist.
Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{21}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}}.

On
\mathcal{T}_{22}\cap\mathcal{A}
, we have
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}\neq{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
by implication (ii) of amenability. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{1}
and
\pi(f_{2})
. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{22}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle~~~+d_{1}+\beta(2d_{2}+d^{*})-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta+\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}}.

Summarizing, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+2\beta+2\rho\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-\beta-2\rho\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-3\beta)\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=(1.4+0.4\rho)\,d^{*}-(1.8-0.4\rho)\,d_{1}+0.4\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta+\beta+\rho\beta)\,d^{*}-(1-\rho\beta)\,d_{1}-2\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=(1.8+0.2\rho)\,d^{*}-(1-0.2\rho)\,d_{1}-0.4\,d_{2}

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{11}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(2+\beta+\rho)\,d^{*}-(2-3\beta-\rho)\,d_{1}-2\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=(2.2+\rho)\,d^{*}-(1.4-\rho)\,d_{1}-0.4\,d_{2}

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{12}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta+2\beta+2\rho\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-2\rho\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-3\beta)\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=(2+0.4\rho)\,d^{*}-(2-0.4\rho)\,d_{1}+0.4\,d_{2}

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{21}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.6\,d^{*}-d_{1}-0.2\,d_{2}

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{22}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta+\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}

\displaystyle=1.8\,d^{*}-d_{1}

Now we combine these inequalities to get an upper bound for
{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)
. When
\rho(f^{*})\leq\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}
, we have
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{1}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}
and
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{2}]=0
. Therefore,

{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot\max\{{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{11}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c),{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{12}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

If the maximum is attained at
{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{11}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
, we have

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{11}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(1.8+0.7\rho)d^{*}-(1.6-0.7\rho)d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(1.8+0.7\times\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}})d^{*}-(1.6-0.7\times\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}})d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle=\boxed{2.26667d^{*}-1.13333d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

If the maximum is attained at
{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{12}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
, we have

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{12}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(1.7+0.4\rho)d^{*}-(1.9-0.4\rho)d_{1}+0.4d_{2}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

(
d_{2}\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot(d^{*}+\rho(d^{*}+d_{1}))+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot\alpha d_{1}
)

\displaystyle\leq(1.9+0.6\rho)d^{*}-(1.3-0.6\rho)d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(1.9+0.6\times\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}})d^{*}-(1.3-0.6\times\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}})d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle=\boxed{2.3\,d^{*}-0.9\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

When
\rho(f^{*})>\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}
, we apply
Lemma

D.2
to combine the inequalities. Note that our bound for
{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{21}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
is smaller than our bound for
{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{22}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
, so we only need to consider cases where the maximum in
Lemma

D.2
is attained at either
{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{11}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{21}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
or
{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{12}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{22}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
.

When
\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}<\rho(f^{*})\leq\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}
, we have
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}]=0,\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{1}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{2}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
. If the maximum in
Lemma

D.2
is attained at
{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{11}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{21}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
, we have

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot({\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{11}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{21}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c))+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(1.65+0.45\rho)\,d^{*}-(1.5-0.45\rho)\,d_{1}+0.05\,d_{2}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

(
d_{2}\leq\alpha d_{1}
)

\displaystyle\leq(1.65+0.45\rho)\,d^{*}-(1.35-0.45\rho)\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(1.65+0.45\times\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})d^{*}-(1.35-0.45\times\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle=\boxed{1.9875d^{*}-1.0125d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

If the maximum in
Lemma

D.2
is attained at
{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{12}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{22}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
, we have

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot({\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{12}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{22}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c))+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(1.65+0.3\rho)\,d^{*}-(1.65-0.3\rho)\,d_{1}+0.3\,d_{2}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

(
d_{2}\leq d^{*}+\rho(d^{*}+d_{1})
)

\displaystyle\leq(1.95+0.6\rho)\,d^{*}-(1.65-0.6\rho)\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(1.95+0.6\times\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})d^{*}-(1.65-0.6\times\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle=\boxed{2.4d^{*}-1.2d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

When
\rho(f^{*})>\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}
, we have
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}]=\nicefrac{{5}}{{4}}-\rho,\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}]=\rho-\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}},\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{1}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{2}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
. If the maximum in
Lemma

D.2
is attained at
{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{11}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{21}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
, we have

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq(\nicefrac{{5}}{{4}}-\rho)\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+(\rho-\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot({\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{11}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{21}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c))+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(1.35+\rho-0.2\rho^{2})\,d^{*}-(2.1-1.4\rho+0.2\rho^{2})\,d_{1}+(0.65-0.8\rho)\,d_{2}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

When
\rho<0.8125=\nicefrac{{0.65}}{{0.8}}
, we use
d_{2}\leq\alpha d_{1}
:

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq(1.35+\rho-0.2\rho^{2})\,d^{*}-(0.15+\rho+0.2\rho^{2})\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(1.35+0.8125-0.2\times 0.8125^{2})d^{*}-(0.15+\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}+0.2\times(\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})^{2})d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle=\boxed{2.03047d^{*}-1.0125d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

When
\rho\geq 0.8125
, we use
d_{2}\geq d_{1}
:

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq(1.35+\rho-0.2\rho^{2})\,d^{*}-(1.45-0.6\rho+0.2\rho^{2})\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(1.35+1-0.2)d^{*}-(1.45-0.6+0.2)d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle=\boxed{2.15\,d^{*}-1.05\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

If the maximum in
Lemma

D.2
is attained at
{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{12}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{22}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
, we have

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq(\nicefrac{{5}}{{4}}-\rho)\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+(\rho-\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot({\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{12}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{22}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c))+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(1.35+0.85\rho-0.2\rho^{2})\,d^{*}-(2.25-1.25\rho+0.2\rho^{2})\,d_{1}+(0.9-0.8\rho)\,d_{2}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

(
d_{2}\leq d^{*}+\rho(d^{*}+d_{1})
)

\displaystyle\leq(2.25+0.95\rho-\rho^{2})\,d^{*}-(2.25-2.15\rho+\rho^{2})\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(2.25+0.95\times\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}-(\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})^{2})d^{*}-(2.25-2.15+1)d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle=\boxed{2.4d^{*}-1.1d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

D.4
Proof of (
6.20
): Clients of Type
\mathsf{C}

In this section, we show that for any client
c
with type
\mathsf{C}
, we have

\Delta_{\mathcal{A}}({c})\leq 2.2\,d^{*}(c)-0.8888\,d_{1}(c).

If the client
c
satisfies
\rho(f^{*})\leq\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}
, we have the same bound as in the type
\mathsf{A}
case in
Section

D.2.1
, where our analysis was independent of whether
f_{2}=\eta_{2}
or not. That is

{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq\boxed{2.1\,d^{*}(c)-0.9\,d_{1}(c)}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

We thus focus on clients with
\rho(f^{*})>\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}
. Compared to our analysis for other client types, our analysis for type
\mathsf{C}
involves a larger neighborhood of the client. In particular, the optimal facility
g^{*}:=\pi(f_{1})
and the local facilities close to it play a crucial role in our analysis. This makes it important to consider finer-grained events. Recall that we used
\mathcal{S}_{1},\mathcal{S}_{2},\mathcal{T}_{1},\mathcal{T}_{2}
to denote simple/tree events restricted to
f^{*}
pointing to
\eta_{1}
or
\eta_{2}
. We now also define events
\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\prime},\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\prime},\mathcal{T}_{1}^{\prime},\mathcal{T}_{2}^{\prime}
similarly, except that they depend on where
g^{*}
points to, rather than
f^{*}
. We classify clients into subtypes according to the characteristics of the swap sets generated on these events:

Claim D.3

(Subtypes within type
\mathsf{C}
)
.

For a client
c
of type
\mathsf{C}
, one of the following is true:

1.

f_{1}
is heavy.

2.

f_{2}
is heavy.

3.

A facility
h
is open near
c
after the simple swap closing
f_{1}
. Formally, a facility
h\neq f_{2}
is open after swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
at distance
d(c,h)\leq 3d_{1}+2d^{*}
on
\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{A}
.

4.

g^{*}\neq f^{*}
,
\rho(g^{*})>\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}
, and for all
b=1,2
, any swap set
\mathcal{P}
generated on
\mathcal{S}_{b}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{A}
, a facility
h\neq f_{2}
is open after swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
at distance
d(c,h)\leq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}2d_{1}+d^{*},&\textup{if}~b=1\\
2d_{1}+d^{*}+\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}(d_{1}+d^{*}),&\textup{if}~b=2\end{array}\right.
.

5.

For any swap set
\mathcal{P}
generated on
\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}
,
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
closes both
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
.

6.

g^{*}\neq f^{*}
,
\rho(g^{*})>\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}
, and there exists
b\in\{1,2\}
such that for any swap set
\mathcal{P}
generated on
\mathcal{T}_{b}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{A}
,
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
closes both
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
.

We prove this claim in
Section

E.5
. Below we present our bounds for each of these subtypes.

D.4.1
When
f_{1}
is a heavy facility

f_{1}
being heavy implies that the swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
doesn’t exist. We thus focus on
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
and
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
.

Simple swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{1}

By implication (Siv) of amenability, we have
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}\neq{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
, and on swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{1}
. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle+(1+\alpha\beta)d_{1}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta)d^{*}-(1-\alpha\beta)d_{1}-2\beta d_{2}}.

Tree swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{1}

Let us first assume
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}\neq{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
, and on swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{1}
and
\pi(f_{2})
by implication (Tii) of amenability. Note that
d(c,\pi(f_{2}))\leq 2d_{2}+d^{*}
by (
3
). Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle+d_{1}+\beta(2d_{2}+d^{*})-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta+\beta)d^{*}-d_{1}}.

The inequality also holds when
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
since our bound for
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}}
does not require
f_{2}
to remain open after the swap.

Simple & tree swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{2}

We have
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
by implication (ii) of amenability. On that swap, the client can be served by
f^{*}
. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta)d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}},

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta)d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}}.

Summarizing, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-(1-\alpha\beta)d_{1}-2\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.6\,d^{*}-0.4d_{1}-0.4\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.6\,d^{*}-d_{1}-0.2\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta+\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}

\displaystyle=1.8\,d^{*}-d_{1}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.6\,d^{*}-d_{1}-0.2\,d_{2}

We now combine these inequalities to get an upper bound for
{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)
.

When
\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}<\rho(f^{*})\leq\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}
, we have
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}]=0,\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{1}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{2}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq 1.65\,d^{*}-0.7\,d_{1}-0.25\,d_{2}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

(
d_{2}\geq d_{1}
)

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{1.65\,d^{*}-0.95\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

When
\rho(f^{*})>\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}
, we have
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}]=\nicefrac{{5}}{{4}}-\rho,\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}]=\rho-\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}},\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{1}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{2}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq(\nicefrac{{5}}{{4}}-\rho)\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+(\rho-\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq 1.65\,d^{*}-(0.6\rho+0.25)\,d_{1}-(0.4-0.2\rho)\,d_{2}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

(
d_{2}\geq d_{1}
)

\displaystyle\leq 1.65\,d^{*}-(0.4\rho+0.65)\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq 1.65\,d^{*}-(0.4\times\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}+0.65)\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle=\boxed{1.65\,d^{*}-0.95\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

D.4.2
When
f_{2}
is a heavy facility

f_{2}
being heavy implies that
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
does not exist. We thus focus on
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
and
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
.

Simple & tree swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{1}

We have
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
by implication (ii) of amenability. On that swap, the client can be served by
f^{*}
. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta)d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}},

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta)d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}}.

Simple swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{2}

Implication (Siv) of amenability implies that
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}\neq{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{2}
. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle+(1+\alpha\beta)d_{2}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(D.52)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta)d^{*}-2\,d_{1}+(1+\alpha\beta-2\beta)d_{2}}.

Tree swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{2}

We first assume that
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}\neq{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{2}
and
\pi(f_{1})
, by implication (Tii) of amenability. Note that
d(c,\pi(f_{1}))\leq d_{1}+d(f_{1},\pi(f_{1}))\leq 2d_{1}+d^{*}
. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle+d_{2}+\beta(2d_{1}+d^{*})-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta+\beta)d^{*}-(2-2\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-2\beta)d_{2}}.

The above inequality also holds when
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
because our bound for
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}}
does not require
f_{1}
to remain open after the swap.
and
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
is non-negative.

Summarizing, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.6\,d^{*}-d_{1}-0.2\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-2\,d_{1}+(1+\alpha\beta-2\beta)\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.6\,d^{*}-2\,d_{1}+1.2\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.6\,d^{*}-d_{1}-0.2\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta+\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-2\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-2\beta)\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.8\,d^{*}-1.6\,d_{1}+0.6\,d_{2}

We now combine these inequalities to get an upper bound for
{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)
.

When
\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}<\rho(f^{*})\leq\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}
, we have
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}]=0,\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{1}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{2}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{1.65\,d^{*}-1.15\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

When
\rho(f^{*})>\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}
, we have
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}]=\nicefrac{{5}}{{4}}-\rho,\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}]=\rho-\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}},\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{1}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{2}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq(\nicefrac{{5}}{{4}}-\rho)\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+(\rho-\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq 1.65\,d^{*}-(\rho+0.4)\,d_{1}+(1.4\rho-1.05)\,d_{2}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

(
\rho>\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}
and
d_{2}\leq d^{*}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{\rho}}(d^{*}+d_{1})
)

\displaystyle\leq(2+1.4\rho-1.05/\rho)\,d^{*}-(\rho+1.05/\rho-1)\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(2+1.4-1.05)\,d^{*}-(1+1.05-1)\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle=\boxed{2.35\,d^{*}-1.05\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

D.4.3
There exists
h
such that
d(c,h)\leq 3d_{1}+2d^{*}
in simple swaps

Simple swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{1}

By implications (ii) and (Siv) of amenablity, we know
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}\neq{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{1}
. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle+(1+\alpha\beta)\,d_{1}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-(1-\alpha\beta)\,d_{1}-2\beta\,d_{2}}.

Simple swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{2}

By implications (ii) and (Siv) of amenablity, we know
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}\neq{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{2}
and
h
. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle+d_{2}+\beta(3d_{1}+2d^{*})-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta+2\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-3\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-2\beta)\,d_{2}}.

Tree swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{1}

By implication (ii) of amenability, we know
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
. Let us first assume that
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}\neq{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{1}
and
\pi(f_{2})
by implication (Tii) of amenability. We have
d(c,\pi(f_{2}))\leq 2d_{2}+d^{*}
by (
3
). Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle+d_{1}+\beta(2d_{2}+d^{*})-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta+\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}}.

This inequality also holds when
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
, because our bound for
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
does not require
f_{2}
to remain open after the swap
and
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
is non-negative.

Tree swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{2}

By implication (ii) of amenability, we know
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
. Again, let us first assume that
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}\neq{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{2}
and
\pi(f_{1})
by implication (ii) of amenability. We have
d(c,\pi(f_{1}))\leq 2d_{1}+d^{*}
by (
2
). Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle+d_{2}+\beta(2d_{1}+d^{*})-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta+\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-2\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-2\beta)\,d_{2}}.

This inequality also holds when
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, because our bound for
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
does not require
f_{1}
to remain open after the swap
and
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
is non-negative.

Summarizing, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-(1-\alpha\beta)\,d_{1}-2\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.6\,d^{*}-0.4\,d_{1}-0.4\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta+2\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-3\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-2\beta)\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=2d^{*}-1.4\,d_{1}+0.6\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta+\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}

\displaystyle=1.8\,d^{*}-d_{1}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta+\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-2\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-2\beta)\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.8\,d^{*}-1.6\,d_{1}+0.6\,d_{2}

We now combine these inequalities to get an upper bound for
{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)
.

When
\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}<\rho(f^{*})\leq\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}
, we have
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}]=0,\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{1}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{2}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq 1.7\,d^{*}-0.85\,d_{1}-0.05\,d_{2}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

(
d_{2}\geq d_{1}
)

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{1.7\,d^{*}-0.9\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

When
\rho(f^{*})>\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}
, we have
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}]=\nicefrac{{5}}{{4}}-\rho,\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}]=\rho-\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}},\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{1}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{2}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq(\nicefrac{{5}}{{4}}-\rho)\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+(\rho-\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(1.4+0.4\rho)\,d^{*}-(0.1+\rho)\,d_{1}+(\rho-0.8)\,d_{2}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

When
\rho\leq 0.8
, we use
d_{2}\geq d_{1}
:

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq(1.4+0.4\rho)\,d^{*}-0.9\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{1.72\,d^{*}-0.9\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

When
\rho>0.8
, we use
d_{2}\leq d^{*}+d(f^{*},d_{2})\leq d^{*}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{\rho}}\cdot(d^{*}+d_{1})
:

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq(1.6+1.4\rho-0.8/\rho)\,d^{*}-(\rho+0.8/\rho-0.9)\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(1.6+1.4-0.8)\,d^{*}-(2\sqrt{0.8}-0.9)\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{2.2\,d^{*}-0.88885\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

D.4.4
d(c,h)\leq 2d_{1}+d^{*}
or
d(c,h)\leq 2d_{1}+d^{*}+\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}(d^{*}+d_{1})
in simple swaps

We have the same bound for
{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)
in this case as the previous case. Indeed, our previous bounds for
{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c),{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
and
{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
remain valid. We replace our bound for
{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
by a bound for

(D.53)

{\delta}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c):=\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\prime}|\mathcal{S}_{2}]{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\prime}|\mathcal{S}_{2}]{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c).

We show that we can upper-bound
{\delta}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
by the same expression as in (
D.52
). Our previous bound for
{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
is linear in
d(c,h)
with a non-negative coefficient:
{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq A\cdot d(c,h)+B
with
A\geq 0
, so

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq A\cdot(2d_{1}+d^{*})+B

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq A\cdot(2d_{1}+d^{*}+\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}(d^{*}+d_{1}))+B.

Plugging them into (
D.53
), we have

\displaystyle{\delta}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq A\cdot(\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}^{\prime}|\mathcal{S}_{2}]\cdot(2d_{1}+d^{*})+\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}^{\prime}|\mathcal{S}_{2}]\cdot(2d_{1}+d^{*}+\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}(d^{*}+d_{1})))+B

\displaystyle\leq A\cdot(\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot(2d_{1}+d^{*})+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot(2d_{1}+d^{*}+\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}(d^{*}+d_{1})))+B

\displaystyle\leq A\cdot(3d_{1}+2d^{*})+B.

D.4.5
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
closes

f_{1}
and
f_{2}
on

\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}

If
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{1}
, we get the same bounds as before:

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-(1-\alpha\beta)\,d_{1}-2\beta\,d_{2}},

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta+\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}}.

We continue to bound
{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
and
{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
.

Simple swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{2}

By implications (ii) and (Siv), we have
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}\neq{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{2}
. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle+(1+\alpha\beta)\,d_{2}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-2\,d_{1}+(1+\alpha\beta-2\beta)\,d_{2}}.

Tree swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{2}

On
\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}
, we know
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
closes both
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}}.

Summarizing, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-(1-\alpha\beta)\,d_{1}-2\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.6\,d^{*}-0.4\,d_{1}-0.4\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-2\,d_{1}+(1+\alpha\beta-2\beta)\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.6\,d^{*}-2\,d_{1}+1.2\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta+\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}

\displaystyle=1.8\,d^{*}-d_{1}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.6\,d^{*}-d_{1}-0.2\,d_{2}

We now combine these inequalities to get an upper bound for
{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)
.

When
\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}<\rho(f^{*})\leq\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}
, we have
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}]=0,\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{1}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{2}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq 1.65\,d^{*}-0.7\,d_{1}-0.25\,d_{2}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

(
d_{2}\geq d_{1}
)

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{1.65\,d^{*}-0.95\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

When
\rho(f^{*})>\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}
, we have
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}]=\nicefrac{{5}}{{4}}-\rho,\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}]=\rho-\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}},\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{1}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{2}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq(\nicefrac{{5}}{{4}}-\rho)\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+(\rho-\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq 1.65\,d^{*}-(1.6\rho-0.5)\,d_{1}+(1.6\rho-1.45)\,d_{2}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

When
\rho\leq 1.45/1.6
, we use
d_{2}\geq d_{1}
:

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{1.65\,d^{*}-0.95\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

When
\rho>1.45/1.6
, we use
d_{2}\leq d^{*}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{\rho}}\cdot(d^{*}+d_{1})
:

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq(1.8+1.6\rho-1.45/\rho)\,d^{*}-(1.6\rho+1.45/\rho-2.1)\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(1.8+1.6-1.45)\,d^{*}-(2\sqrt{1.6\times 1.45}-2.1)\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{1.95\,d^{*}-0.94630\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

D.4.6
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
closes
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
on

\mathcal{T}_{b}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{A}
for some
b\in\{1,2\}

Bounds for simple swaps remain the same as before:

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-(1-\alpha\beta)\,d_{1}-2\beta\,d_{2}},

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-2\,d_{1}+(1+\alpha\beta-2\beta)\,d_{2}}.

For tree swaps, we partition
\mathcal{T}\cap\mathcal{A}
as the union of
\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{T}_{3-b}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{T}_{3-b}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{T}_{b}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{A}
. On the first two events, our bounds are the same as in
Section

D.4.3
:

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{T}_{3-b}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta+\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}},

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{T}_{3-b}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta+\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-2\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-2\beta)\,d_{2}}.

On
\mathcal{T}_{b}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{A}
, we have
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
closes both
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{b}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}}.

Summarizing, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-(1-\alpha\beta)\,d_{1}-2\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.6\,d^{*}-0.4\,d_{1}-0.4\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-2\,d_{1}+(1+\alpha\beta-2\beta)\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.6\,d^{*}-2\,d_{1}+1.2\,d_{2}

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{T}_{3-b}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta+\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}

\displaystyle=1.8\,d^{*}-d_{1}

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{T}_{3-b}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta+\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-2\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-2\beta)\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.8\,d^{*}-1.6\,d_{1}+0.6\,d_{2}

\displaystyle{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{b}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.6\,d^{*}-d_{1}-0.2\,d_{2}

We now combine these inequalities to get an upper bound for
{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)
.

When
\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}<\rho(f^{*})\leq\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}
, we have
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}]=0,\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{T}_{3-b}^{\prime}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{T}_{3-b}^{\prime}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{8}},\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{b}^{\prime}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{8}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{T}_{3-b}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{8}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{T}_{3-b}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{b}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq 1.65\,d^{*}-0.775\,d_{1}-0.175\,d_{2}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

(
d_{2}\geq d_{1}
)

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{1.65\,d^{*}-0.95\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

When
\rho(f^{*})>\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}
, we have
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}]=\nicefrac{{5}}{{4}}-\rho,\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}]=\rho-\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}},\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{T}_{3-b}^{\prime}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{T}_{3-b}^{\prime}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{8}},\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{b}^{\prime}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq(\nicefrac{{5}}{{4}}-\rho)\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+(\rho-\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{8}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{T}_{3-b}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{8}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{T}_{3-b}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle~~~+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{b}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq 1.65\,d^{*}-(1.6\rho-0.425)\,d_{1}+(1.6\rho-1.375)\,d_{2}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

When
\rho\leq 1.375/1.6
, we use
d_{2}\geq d_{1}
:

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{1.65\,d^{*}-0.95\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

When
\rho>1.375/1.6
, we use
d_{2}\leq d^{*}+\nicefrac{{1}}{{\rho}}(d^{*}+d_{1})
:

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq(1.875+1.6\rho-1.375/\rho)\,d^{*}-(1.6\rho+1.375/\rho-2.025)\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(1.875+1.6-1.375)\,d^{*}-(2\sqrt{1.6\times 1.375}-2.025)\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{2.1\,d^{*}-0.94147\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

D.5
Proof of (
6.21
): Clients of Type
\mathsf{D}

In this section, we show that for any client
c
with type
\mathsf{D}
, we have

\Delta_{\mathcal{A}}({c})\leq 2.5203\,d^{*}(c)-0.8888\,d_{1}(c).

Similar to
D.3
for type
\mathsf{C}
clients, we also have the following claim classifying type
\mathsf{D}
clients into subtypes. The only change is in item (e), where we replace
\mathcal{T}_{2}
by
\mathcal{T}_{1}
because the roles of
\eta_{1}
and
\eta_{2}
are now swapped.

Claim D.4

(Type
\mathsf{D}
subcases)
.

For a client
c
of type
\mathsf{D}
, one of the following is true:

1.

f_{1}
is heavy.

2.

f_{2}
is heavy.

3.

A facility
h
is open near
c
after the simple swap closing
f_{1}
. Formally, a facility
h\neq f_{2}
is open after swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
at distance
d(c,h)\leq 3d_{1}+2d^{*}
on
\mathcal{S}\cap\mathcal{A}
.

4.

g^{*}\neq f^{*}
,
\rho(g^{*})>\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}
, and for all
b=1,2
, any swap set
\mathcal{P}
generated on
\mathcal{S}_{b}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{A}
, a facility
h\neq f_{2}
is open after swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
at distance
d(c,h)\leq\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}2d_{1}+d^{*},&\textup{if}~b=1\\
2d_{1}+d^{*}+\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}(d_{1}+d^{*}),&\textup{if}~b=2\end{array}\right.
.

5.

For any swap set
\mathcal{P}
generated on
\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}
,
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
closes both
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
;

6.

g^{*}\neq f^{*}
,
\rho(g^{*})>\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}
, and there exists
b\in\{1,2\}
such that for any swap set
\mathcal{P}
generated on
\mathcal{T}_{b}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{A}
,
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
closes both
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
.

D.5.1
When
f_{1}
is a heavy facility

f_{1}
being heavy implies that
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
doesn’t exist. We thus focus on
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
and
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
.

Simple & tree swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{1}

We have
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
by implication (ii) of amenability. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta)d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}},

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta)d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}}.

Simple swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{2}

By implication (Siv) of amenability, we have
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}\neq{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
the client can be served by
f^{*}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{1}
. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle+(1+\alpha\beta)d_{1}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta)d^{*}-(1-\alpha\beta)\,d_{1}-2\beta\,d_{2}}

Tree swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{2}

Let us first assume that
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}\neq{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{1}
and
\pi(f_{2})
, by implication (Tii) of amenability.
We have
d(c,\pi(f_{2}))\leq\leq 2d_{2}+d^{*}
by (
3
). Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle+d_{1}+\beta(2d_{2}+d^{*})-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta+\beta)d^{*}-d_{1}}

This inequality also holds when
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
, because our bound for
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}}
does not require
f_{2}
to remain open after the swap and
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
is non-negative.

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.6\,d^{*}-d_{1}-0.2\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-(1-\alpha\beta)\,d_{1}-2\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.6\,d^{*}-0.4\,d_{1}-0.4\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.6\,d^{*}-d_{1}-0.2\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta+\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}

\displaystyle=1.8\,d^{*}-d_{1}

We now combine these inequalities to get an upper bound for
{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)
.

When
\rho(f^{*})\leq\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}
, we have
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{1}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{2}]=0
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

(
d_{1}\leq d_{2}
)

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{1.6\,d^{*}-1.2\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

When
\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}<\rho(f^{*})\leq\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}
, we have
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}]=0,\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{1}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{2}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq 1.65\,d^{*}-d_{1}-0.15\,d_{2}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

(
d_{1}\leq d_{2}
)

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{1.65\,d^{*}-1.15\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

When
\rho(f^{*})>\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}
, we have
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}]=\nicefrac{{5}}{{4}}-\rho,\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}]=\rho-\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}},\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{1}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{2}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq(\nicefrac{{5}}{{4}}-\rho)\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+(\rho-\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq 1.65\,d^{*}-(1.45-0.6\rho)\,d_{1}-(0.2\rho)\,d_{2}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

(
d_{1}\leq d_{2}
)

\displaystyle\leq 1.65\,d^{*}-(1.45-0.4\rho)\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{1.65\,d^{*}-1.05\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

D.5.2
When
f_{2}
is a heavy facility

f_{2}
being heavy implies that the swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
doesn’t exist. We thus focus on
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
and
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
.

Simple swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{1}

We have
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}\neq{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
by implication (Siv) of amenability. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
and
f_{1}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{2}
. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle d^{*}+\beta d_{1}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle+(1+\alpha\beta)d_{2}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\boxed{d^{*}-(2-\beta)\,d_{1}+(1+\alpha\beta-2\beta)d_{2}}.

We can also use
(1-\beta)d^{*}+2\beta\,d_{1}
to upper-bound
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}}
(by (
2
)) and get

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1-\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-2\beta)\,d_{1}+(1+\alpha\beta-2\beta)d_{2}}.

Tree swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{1}

Let us first assume that
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}\neq{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
and
f_{1}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{2}
and
\pi(f_{1})
by implication (Tii) of amenability. We have
d(c,\pi(f_{1}))\leq 2d_{1}+d^{*}
by (
2
). Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle d^{*}+\beta\,d_{1}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle+d_{2}+\beta(2d_{1}+d^{*})-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\beta)d^{*}-(2-3\beta)d_{1}+(1-2\beta)\,d_{2}}.

If
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}
, we still have the same bound:

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle d^{*}+\beta\,d_{2}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(non-negative terms)

\displaystyle+(1-2\beta)(d_{2}-d_{1})+\beta\,d^{*}+\beta\,d_{1}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle(1+\beta)d^{*}-(2-3\beta)d_{1}+(1-2\beta)\,d_{2}.

Simple & tree swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{2}

We have
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
by implication (ii) of amenability. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta)d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}},

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle d^{*}+\beta d_{2}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\boxed{d^{*}-d_{1}}.

Summarizing, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle d^{*}-(2-\beta)\,d_{1}+(1+\alpha\beta-2\beta)\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=d^{*}-1.8\,d_{1}+1.2\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1-\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-2\beta)\,d_{1}+(1+\alpha\beta-2\beta)d_{2}

\displaystyle=0.8\,d^{*}-1.6\,d_{1}+1.2\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.6\,d^{*}-d_{1}-0.2\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\beta)d^{*}-(2-3\beta)d_{1}+(1-2\beta)d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.2\,d^{*}-1.4\,d_{1}+0.6\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle d^{*}-d_{1}

\displaystyle=d^{*}-d_{1}

We now combine these inequalities to get an upper bound for
{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)
.

When
\rho(f^{*})\leq\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}
, we have
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{1}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{2}]=0
. In this case we use the second inequality for
\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq d^{*}-1.5\,d_{1}+0.9\,d_{2}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

(
d_{2}\leq d^{*}+\rho(d^{*}+d_{1})
)

\displaystyle\leq(1.9+0.9\rho)\,d^{*}-(1.5-0.9\rho)\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(1.9+0.9\cdot\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}})d^{*}-(1.5-0.9\cdot\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}})\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{2.5\,d^{*}-0.9\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

When
\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}<\rho(f^{*})\leq\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}
, we have
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}]=0,\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{1}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{2}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
. In this case we use the first inequality for
\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq 1.05\,d^{*}-1.5\,d_{1}+0.75\,d_{2}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

(
d_{2}\leq d^{*}+\rho(d^{*}+d_{1})
)

\displaystyle\leq(1.8+0.75\rho)\,d^{*}-(1.5-0.75\rho)\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(1.8+0.75\cdot\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})-(1.5-0.75\cdot\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{2.3625\,d^{*}-0.9375\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

When
\rho(f^{*})>\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}
, we have
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}]=\nicefrac{{5}}{{4}}-\rho,\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}]=\rho-\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}},\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{1}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{2}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
.
In this case we use the first inequality for
\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)

\displaystyle\leq(\nicefrac{{5}}{{4}}-\rho)\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+(\rho-\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\cdot{\delta}_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(0.6+0.6\rho)\,d^{*}-(2.1-0.8\rho)\,d_{1}+(1.8-1.4\rho)\,d_{2}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

(
d_{2}\leq d^{*}+\rho(d^{*}+d_{1})
)

\displaystyle\leq(2.4+\rho-1.4\rho^{2})\,d^{*}-(2.1-2.6\rho+1.4\rho^{2})\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq(2.4+\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}}-1.4(\nicefrac{{3}}{{4}})^{2})\,d^{*}-(2.1-2.6\cdot\nicefrac{{13}}{{14}}+1.4(\nicefrac{{13}}{{14}})^{2})\,d_{1}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\boxed{2.3625\,d^{*}-0.8928\,d_{1}}+O(\varepsilon)(d^{*}+d_{1}).

D.5.3
There exists a facility
h
such that
d(c,h)\leq 3d_{1}+2d^{*}
in simple swaps

Simple swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{1}

Implications (ii) and (Siv) of amenability imply
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}\neq{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
.
On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
and
f_{1}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{2}
and
h
. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle d^{*}+\beta\,d_{1}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle+d_{2}+\beta(3d_{1}+2d^{*})-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+2\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-4\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-2\beta)\,d_{2}}.

In
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
, we can use
0.776(1+\alpha\beta)d^{*}+0.224(d^{*}+\beta\,d_{1})
instead of
d^{*}+\beta\,d_{1}
. This gives

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+2\beta+0.776\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-3.224\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-2\beta)\,d_{2}}.

Simple swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{2}

Implications (ii) and (Siv) of amenability imply
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}\neq{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{1}
. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle+(1+\alpha\beta)\,d_{1}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-(1-\alpha\beta)\,d_{1}-2\beta\,d_{2}}.

Tree swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{1}

We have
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
by implication (ii) of amenability. Let us first assume that
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}\neq{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{2}
and
\pi(f_{1})
by implication (Tii) of amenability.
Note that
d(c,\pi(f_{1}))\leq 2d_{1}+d^{*}
by (
2
). Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle+d_{2}+\beta(2d_{1}+d^{*})-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta+\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-2\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-2\beta)\,d_{2}}.

This inequality also holds when
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, because our bound for
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
does not require
f_{1}
to remain open after the swap
and
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
is non-negative.

In
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
, we can use
d^{*}+\beta\,d_{1}
instead of
(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}
. This gives

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-3\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-2\beta)\,d_{2}}.

This bound also holds when
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
because in this case we have

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{2},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle d^{*}+\beta\,d_{1}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(non-negative terms)

\displaystyle+(1-\beta)(d_{2}-d_{1})+\beta d_{1}+\beta d^{*}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle(1+\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-3\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-2\beta)\,d_{2}.

Tree swaps with
\tau(f^{*})=\eta_{2}

We have
{\langle\!\langle f^{*}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
by implication (ii) of amenability. Let us first assume that
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}\neq{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f^{*}
. On swap
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}
, the client can be served by
f_{1}
and
\pi(f_{2})
by implication (Tii) of amenability. Note that
d(c,\pi(f_{2}))\leq d_{2}+d(f_{2},\pi(f_{2}))\leq d_{2}+d(f_{2},f^{*})\leq d_{2}+\rho d(f_{1},f^{*})\leq d_{2}+\rho(d_{1}+d^{*})
. Therefore,

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq{}

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

(
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
)

\displaystyle+d_{1}+\beta(d_{2}+\rho(d^{*}+d_{1}))-d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\boxed{(1+\alpha\beta+\rho\beta)\,d^{*}-(1-\rho\beta)\,d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}}.

This inequality also holds when
{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}={\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}
, because our bound for
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle f^{*},\neg f_{1}\rangle\!\rangle}}
does not require
f_{2}
to remain open after the swap and
{\delta}_{{\langle\!\langle\neg f_{2}\rangle\!\rangle}}
is non-negative.

Summarizing, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+2\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-4\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-2\beta)\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.4\,d^{*}-1.2\,d_{1}+0.6\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+2\beta+0.776\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-3.224\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-2\beta)\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.8656\,d^{*}-1.3552\,d_{1}+0.6\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{S}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta)\,d^{*}-(1-\alpha\beta)\,d_{1}-2\beta\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.6\,d^{*}-0.4\,d_{1}-0.4\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta+\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-2\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-2\beta)\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.8\,d^{*}-1.6\,d_{1}+0.6\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{1}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\beta)\,d^{*}-(2-3\beta)\,d_{1}+(1-2\beta)\,d_{2}

\displaystyle=1.2\,d^{*}-1.4\,d_{1}+0.6\,d_{2}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{T}_{2}\cap\mathcal{A}}(c)\leq

\displaystyle(1+\alpha\beta+\rho\beta)\,d^{*}-(1-\rho\beta)\,d_{1}-\beta\,d_{2}\quad\quad\quad\quad=(1.6+0.2\rho)\,d^{*}-(1-0.2\rho)\,d_{1}-0.2\,d_{2}

We now combine these inequalities to get an upper bound for
{\Delta}_{\mathcal{A}}(c)
.

When
\rho(f^{*})\leq\nicefrac{{2}}{{3}}
, we have
\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{1}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{1}]=\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\Pr[\mathcal{S}_{2}]=\Pr[\mathcal{T}_{2}]=0
